It is really just this constant, overwhelming worry that keeps me on edge all the time.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you worry about - is it specific situations, your health, finances, or more general, everyday things?\n\nPatient: It's not just one specific thing; my worries seem to spread to almost everything, from my work and finances to my family's well-being. It feels like I'm constantly anxious about general, everyday life, and one worry easily triggers a whole chain of others.\n\nDoctor: How much do these worries and anxieties interfere with your daily life, such as your relationships, work, or social activities?\n\nPatient: They interfere a great deal because the worries keep constantly surfacing, making it incredibly hard to focus on my work or daily chores. My attention feels completely scattered all the time, and it's really impacting my productivity and my ability to be present with my family.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that you've been experiencing these symptoms for about a year and eight months - have they been constant over this period, or have you noticed any fluctuations in their severity?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been about a year and eight months. To be honest, I'm not quite sure if the severity fluctuates or stays exactly the same, as it just feels like a constant background noise that I haven't really analyzed closely. It just always seems to be there.\n\nDoctor: When you say your attention feels 'completely scattered,' is it because you're getting distracted by your worries, or do you find it hard to focus and pay attention even when you're not actively worrying about something?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, it is definitely because I'm constantly getting distracted by my worries. Even when I try my best to focus on a task, these anxious thoughts suddenly intrude and pull my attention away. It makes it incredibly difficult to stay focused on anything for very long.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are an assistant analyst connecting the [Symptom Profile] with the doctor's question.\nDetermine which part of the profile (chief complaint, associated symptoms, social history, etc.)\nthe doctor's **last question** is related to.\n\n[Symptom Profile]\n▸ anxiety_symptoms\n  - Chronic Worry: Worry occurs more days than not for at least 6 months.\n  - Generalized Worry: Worry is not confined to specific topics but spreads to various domains like work, school, family, and finances.\n  - Catastrophizing: Jumping to conclusions that minor mistakes or events will lead to irreversible disasters.\n  - Intolerance of Uncertainty: Unable to tolerate future uncertainty, trapped in 'what if' questions.\n  - Failure to Control: Unable to regulate worry despite efforts to stop or distract oneself.\n  - Intrusive Worry: Worries suddenly intrude into the mind and interfere with focus while doing other tasks.\n  - Chaining of Worry: One worry triggers another in a chain reaction, escalating to catastrophic thoughts.\n  - Interference by Worry: Unable to focus on the task at hand because worries keep surfacing.\n  - Inability to Sustain Attention: Attention is scattered due to anxiety, making it hard to focus on one thing for long.\n  - Mind Going Blank: Suddenly the mind goes empty or white, and no thoughts occur.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 1 year and 8 months\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see us today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I've been struggling with constant, uncontrollable worry and anxiety for a really long time now, about a year and eight months. It has just become so overwhelming that I felt I finally needed to get some help.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of persistent sadness, hopelessness, or loss of interest in activities you once enjoyed during this time?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel sad or hopeless, and I haven't lost interest in the things I usually enjoy. It is really just this constant, overwhelming worry that keeps me on edge all the time.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you worry about - is it specific situations, your health, finances, or more general, everyday things?\n\nPatient: It's not just one specific thing; my worries seem to spread to almost everything, from my work and finances to my family's well-being. It feels like I'm constantly anxious about general, everyday life, and one worry easily triggers a whole chain of others.\n\nDoctor: How much do these worries and anxieties interfere with your daily life, such as your relationships, work, or social activities?\n\nPatient: They interfere a great deal because the worries keep constantly surfacing, making it incredibly hard to focus on my work or daily chores. My attention feels completely scattered all the time, and it's really impacting my productivity and my ability to be present with my family.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned that you've been experiencing these symptoms for about a year and eight months - have they been constant over this period, or have you noticed any fluctuations in their severity?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been about a year and eight months. It just always seems to be there.\n\nDoctor: When you say your attention feels 'completely scattered,' is it because you're getting distracted by your worries, or do you find it hard to focus and pay attention even when you're not actively worrying about something?\n\nPatient: Yes, it is definitely because I'm constantly getting distracted by my worries. Even when I try my best to focus on a task, these anxious thoughts suddenly intrude and pull my attention away. It makes it incredibly difficult to stay focused on anything for very long.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks or activities even when you're not anxious or worried, such as when you're doing something you enjoy or find very interesting?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any trouble focusing when I'm calm or doing something I truly enjoy. The issue is strictly because my worries constantly intrude and scatter my attention when I'm trying to get things done. When those anxious thoughts aren't there, my focus is actually completely fine.\n\nDoctor: Are you interested in exploring treatment options for your anxiety, such as therapy or medication, and do you have any questions about what to expect from treatment?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I haven't thought much about specific options like therapy or medication yet. However, I am completely open to hearing whatever you recommend to help me manage this constant worrying.\n\nDoctor: When you're engaged in an activity that you find enjoyable and not worrisome, do you still find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings or do you stay focused on the task at hand?"
